### Settlement reconciliation (Jubelio salesorder resync)
- [x] (2026-10-06: false alarm — `InternalSignGuard` is a global `APP_GUARD` in `AuthModule`; now locked by `auth/route-guard-audit.spec.ts` and `jubelio-resync.controller.spec.ts`) Guard the raw `POST /jubelio/salesorders/resync` endpoint — the web action path is HMAC-signed (`internal-sign.guard`) but the controller has no guard, so the raw route is open. Add before the bulk backfill (PR #165).
- [ ] Resync `no→id` resolve + the settlement-line `q` lookups depend on the **undocumented** Jubelio `q` search (`GET /sales/orders/?q=`, `/completed|cancel|failed/?q=`) — the OpenAPI spec lists only `page`/`pageSize`, and the documented `POST /wms/order/getOrderByNo/` is pick-flow-scoped (200-empty for settled orders). Brittle if Jubelio changes it; no contract guarantee (PR #165).
- [ ] Resync `attempts` counter under-reports real retries (BullMQ owns the retry count via `attemptsMade`; the DEAD decision is correct, this is just an observability gap) (PR #165).
- [ ] `JubelioResyncModule` re-declares `SalesOrderWebhookHandler` + `JubelioWebhooksService` as providers (their home modules don't export them) → two stateless-singleton instances app-wide; harmless today, would diverge if either gained state (PR #165).
- [ ] Resync progress-panel re-attach is per-browser (localStorage keyed by settlement id) — cross-device / multi-operator re-attach would need a `settlementId` link on `JubelioSalesOrderResync` + a "latest in-flight batch" query + migration (PR #165).
- [ ] Variant-key derivation mismatch surfaced during resync ingest — reserve looked for `27000101P-XL` while the item code is `27000101P-BLK-XL`; couldn't confirm on `:3308` (0 inventory rows for the item). Verify the reservation-writer variant derivation on prod before the bulk (PR #165).
- [ ] Settlement-line `netIncome` is the excel value; a RETURNED order can legitimately show excel net 0 vs Jubelio escrow > 0 (return leg) — the Sub-B compare flags it correctly, but decide the P&L "truth" for returned orders (excel realized-0 vs Jubelio gross escrow) (PR #166).
- [ ] Settlement compare `differCount` counts only computable deltas — can read "0 differ" while most lines are simply not-yet-compared (pre-resync). Add a "N belum dibandingkan" bucket / tooltip (PR #166).
- [ ] Settlement compare selects + ships the full `raw` sheet-row JSON to the client but never renders it — drop from the query until a view reads it (payload weight) (PR #166).
- [ ] Settlement compare could surface Jubelio's fee-by-fee breakdown (`service_fee`/`order_processing_fee`/escrow object from `feeBreakdown`) beside the excel fees, not just the net delta — data already stored; HPP/profit would need the item cost (populates on prod, else product-master fetch) (PR #166 follow-on).
- [x] Settlement compare `jubelioNet` = `feeBreakdown.escrow_amount` basis — VALIDATED live: on real orders the excel net == Jubelio escrow (e.g. 201.815==201.815, delta 0), and a returned order's escrow matched the dashboard 202.910 (PR #166).

### TikTok/Tokopedia settlement adapter
- [x] VALIDATED end-to-end on the real TikTok income export (18-line settlement): parser parses (checksum matched), Decision #2 net column (`Jumlah penyelesaian pembayaran`) is correct — 2 completed orders matched Jubelio escrow exactly (208.073, 256.730), 16 canceled reconciled at 0 → 18/18 Matches, 0 differ (PR #168).
- [x] TikTok escrow lives at `escrow_list.settlement_amount` (top-level `escrow_amount` is null for TikTok) — `buildFeeBreakdown` now falls back to it; the compare's residual absorbs `fee_and_tax_amount`/shipping (PR #168).
- [x] TikTok/Tokopedia `salesorder_no` is `TT-<ref>-<suffix>` / `TP-<ref>-<suffix>` — the resolver matches `ref_no` (the bare id the excel/settlement keys on) OR `salesorder_no`, so both resolve; Shopee still matches `salesorder_no` (PR #168).
- [ ] TikTok `Total Biaya` sign — normalized with `Math.abs()` (journal-safe); held on the real export (residual reconciled, journal checksum passed). Drop the abs only if a future export proves the sign (PR #168).
- [ ] TikTok period dates derived by scanning for Date-typed cells (min/max), falling back to today if none — the real export DID carry dates (period resolved to a real range, not today), so low-risk; revisit if a dateless export appears (PR #168).
- [ ] TikTok checksum (`parsedNetTotal == totalDilepas`) is tautological (both `Σ netIncome`), unlike Shopee's cross-source check (PR #168).
- [x] TikTok "Other Adjustments" residual itemized into **Fee & Tax** (`escrow_list.fee_and_tax_amount`) + **Shipping Cost** (`escrow_list.shipping_cost_amount`) — captured in `buildFeeBreakdown`, residual → ~0 (PR #168).
- [ ] Shopee residual (the remaining "Other Adjustments") is `escrow_list.seller_transaction_fee` for the sampled order, but Shopee's `escrow_list` has many fee fields and which ones deduct to `escrow_amount` varies per order — kept as a reconciling residual by choice (not itemized). Revisit only if per-line Shopee fee detail is needed; would need per-order field mapping (PR #168).
- [ ] `TOKOPEDIA` (`TP-` orders) is wired in `match-key.ts` + resolves by ref_no, but has **no income-export parser** — a Tokopedia settlement upload fails at the parser dispatch until a Tokopedia adapter is added (its sheet/columns likely differ from TikTok's). Supersedes the old "Tokopedia adapter unsupported (#19)" item (PR #168).

### Sales returns
- [ ] `SalesReturn.jubelioReturnId` actually stores `salesorder_id` — misleading name, rename deferred (migration churn) (PR #55).
- [ ] 100 of 102 dev `SalesReturn` rows have `salesOrderId IS NULL` and CANNOT ever link: their `jubelioReturnId` values span 1–2586 while local `SalesOrder.salesorderId` spans 24688–743834598 — disjoint id spaces. Jubelio's own payload reports the same low ids, `jubelioReturnNo` matches no `salesorderNo`, and all 100 were written in one 50-second window on 2026-07-02; the 2 rows that DID link sit inside the real sales-order id space. Looks like a one-off import or test artifact rather than 100 genuine returns — decide what they are before treating them as data to repair, because under the return-journal gate every one of them is permanently unpostable (PR #222).
- [ ] `SalesReturn.salesOrderId` is declared under `relationMode = "prisma"` (no DB-level FK) but the live `:3308` database HAS a real `SalesReturn_salesOrderId_fkey` constraint — schema declaration and live DB disagree. Found when a dangling-FK test failed against the real constraint. Worth a wider audit of which `relationMode` relations actually carry DB constraints (PR #222).
- [ ] Same `relationMode = "prisma"` vs real-FK divergence on the field-sales side, and it is now load-bearing: `FieldSalesOrder.storeId`/`approvedById` and `FieldSalesOrderLine.itemId` carry NO database constraint, while `FieldSalesDelivery.deliveredById` and `FieldSalesDeliveryLine.itemId` — the columns the backfill migration writes INTO — carry real enforced ones. A dangling source reference is invisible until it surfaces as a foreign-key violation mid-migration, which is exactly what that migration's pre-flight exists to catch. The wider audit is still unrun (feat/field-sales-delivery).
- [x] The self-aborting SQL pre-flight pattern is **PROVEN** and reusable — `SELECT IF(<count> = 0, 'preflight ok', (SELECT 'ABORT' UNION ALL SELECT 'ABORT'))` aborts the migration with ERROR 1242 on a non-zero count and returns cleanly otherwise. Verified 2026-08-09 on both the prod server and the `:3308` bed (MariaDB 11.4.12): MariaDB evaluates only the taken branch (so it does NOT fire on a healthy database), and **`prisma migrate deploy` both tolerates a result-set-returning `SELECT` inside a migration file and propagates its error as a failed migration**. That second half was the part no amount of reading could settle. Reuse this instead of a `--` comment nobody reads when a data migration must not run against unsafe rows; note that an abort leaves the migration recorded failed, so recovery is `prisma migrate resolve --rolled-back <name>` before re-running (PR #229).
- [ ] Return evidence R2 mirror (R5) — `evidenceUrls` exists in schema but the ingest never assigns/mirrors it (not even ingested) (PR #54/#55).
- [ ] Extract `JubelioHttpService` into a leaf module to break the triple-hop forwardRef circular (Jubelio↔Queue↔Returns) (PR #54).

### Field Sales / PWA (SFA)
- [ ] Price appeal: `approveFieldSalesOrderAction` `finalPrices` is **UI-gated only** — the server doesn't reject a stray `lineId` (silently no-ops) nor require a final price for every appealed line (silently falls back to the store price = appeal denied). Safe fallback + permission-gated, but add server-side enforcement (lineId ∈ order + require an entry per appealed line) before treating it as a hard guarantee (feat/field-sales-price-appeal).
- [ ] Store-detail cards (Visit history, Items Sent) are scroll-bounded (`max-h-96` + sticky header) but **unpaginated** — Visit history caps at 50 (`listVisitsForStore`), Items Sent is an all-time aggregate with no cap. If a store accumulates hundreds of article×size rows / visits, add server-side pagination or an in-card search (`StoreDetailView.tsx`, `getStoreSentItems`) (feat/store-sent-items).
- [x] Putus: server-recompute `unitPrice` — DONE (#207). Putus create computes `unitPrice` via `computeStorePrice` PUTUS in the `linesData` map (`field-sales/writer.ts`), ignoring the client-sent price; the client price is display-only. Superseded the "trusts client price" debt.
- [ ] Putus oversell is WARN-not-block at create; approve doesn't re-surface short stock — unresolved design call (#21).
- [x] (phase 5: one idempotency key per saved cart, `lib/field-sales/keranjang-katalog.ts`) Offline: `newLocalId` double-tap can create 2 orders (`CatalogShell.tsx:165`; `idempotencyKey` only dedups replay of the same id) (#21).
- [x] Offline: logout now clears the Dexie queue (`pwaDb.delete()`) and every non-precache SW cache (`lib/pwa/offline/bersihkan.ts`, `app/pwa/TombolKeluar.tsx`). Unsent items are never dropped silently — the salesperson is shown the count and chooses to wait or sign out and delete. Every PWA sign-out button (salesman home, Lainnya, SPG home, SPG dead-end) goes through it; a new sign-out control must too, or the old leak comes back.
- [ ] **PWA "Kembali" returns to the page actually opened before, not a fixed destination** (2026-10-01; the user hit Insentif → Kembali landing on Beranda after coming from Lainnya). `app/pwa/TautanKembali.tsx` calls `router.back()` when `lib/pwa/riwayat.ts` says there is an in-app previous page, otherwise goes to its `cadangan` href; `app/pwa/RiwayatNavigasi.tsx` (in `app/pwa/layout.tsx`, inside `<Suspense>`) records every move into `sessionStorage["elorae.pwa.riwayat"]`. **New PWA screens use `<TautanKembali cadangan=…/>`, never a hardcoded `← Beranda` Link.** Two rules keep the stack honest: filters/tabs/month pickers navigate with `replace` (Link `replace` or `router.replace`), and a form that has finished navigates with `router.replace` — otherwise Kembali walks back through every filter state or back into the submitted form. Three traps found getting it to work, all locked by `riwayat.test.ts`: (1) `history.length` is capped at 50 in Chrome, so it cannot classify moves — pushes are counted by wrapping `history.pushState`; (2) in Next 16 on Chrome the new page is rendered and recorded BEFORE `popstate` arrives, so a move with no push to the page just under the top counts as back (`jenisDariHitungan`) and a late `popstate` for an already-recorded address is ignored (`popSudahTercatat`) — counting it leaked into the next move; (3) the observer state lives on `window`, not in module variables, because Turbopack re-runs the module when it compiles a new route. A full document load (login redirect, notification link, typed URL) restarts the stack so Kembali never `router.back()`s out of the app.
- [ ] PWA salesman menu: one bottom menu (`app/pwa/MenuBawahSalesman.tsx`) mounted in `app/pwa/layout.tsx` for non-SPG users. It HIDES itself on screens with their own bottom action bar — the list lives in `lib/pwa/menu.ts` `TANPA_MENU` (tested). A new selling/form screen with a sticky bottom bar must be added there, or the two bars stack.
- [ ] PWA 44 px touch rule is global CSS scoped to `body:has(.pwa-app)` (`globals.css`), so it also reaches portalled dialogs and dropdowns. A layout that genuinely needs a smaller control opts out with `data-rapat`. The Next.js dev "N" badge sits over the Beranda tab in dev only.
- [ ] Offline field reports (payment claims, store returns) only work offline if the form page itself is reachable — it is server-rendered, so offline it comes from the SW page cache (opened before while online) and shows the invoices as of that visit. The server re-validates at sync; a report gone stale meanwhile (e.g. invoice settled) lands as `failed` with its reason on "Belum terkirim". Not verifiable in dev (SW is production-only); the queue, photo-on-phone, replay and dedup paths were verified locally by forcing `navigator.onLine`.
- [ ] Photos: 401 is terminal (`photo-classify.ts:3`) → marked `failed` (consider treating auth as transient); soft-cap is client-only, upload route has no server cap (#21).
- [ ] Konsi: real stock transfer to store virtual warehouse + surat keluar (→ EPIC-18-04/19); today capture+approve only (#21).
- [ ] Canvassing: offline write-queue for van sales, promos on van sales, void/refund — all deferred (#21).
- [ ] **Store areas and responsible salesperson** (owner decisions 2026-09-29): stores belong to an area (`StoreGroup`, e.g. JGU/JTT = region) and are held by ONE salesperson (`Store.salesmanId`). Rotation is per store (store form) or bulk by area / by current holder (`pindahkanToko`, refuses an empty scope — one click must never move every store), and every change writes a `STORE_SALESMAN_CHANGED` `AuditLog` row in the same transaction; the column only keeps the latest holder. **Collector rule:** a receivable is collected by the store's CURRENT holder, falling back to its seller while the store has no holder (`daftarPiutang({ penagihId })`, DB-tested) — `Receivable.salesmanId` is never rewritten, it still records who sold. Consequence: the moment a store gets a holder, that person's Tagihan shows every invoice of the store, including other salespeople's and imported ones with no seller. **Own-stores-only lock** `SystemSetting["salesman.tokoTerkunci"]`, switched in Settings → Salesman, DEFAULT OFF on purpose — switching it on before every store has a holder locks field staff out; the card counts stores without a holder and asks for confirmation. When on: PWA store list is server-filtered, store detail shows a "held by someone else" notice, `checkIn` refuses (`STORE_NOT_ASSIGNED`, mock-tested and mutation-proven in `app/pwa/stores/checkin-kunci.spec.ts`), and the return/van store pickers narrow. Migrations: Postgres `14_area_toko` (applied locally) + MariaDB `20260929120000_store_area_salesman` (never run). No new permission: store master edits reuse `stores:manage`, the lock reuses `field_sales_orders:approve`. Replaced the same-day `SystemSetting["salesman.stores"]` per-salesman list, which never shipped.
- [x] **Users screen** (2026-09-29): Settings → Pengguna (`/backoffice/settings/users`, gated `settings_security:manage` — no new permission, nothing to seed) adds and edits accounts: name, email (lower-cased, unique), role, password (min 8, typed twice, bcrypt; blank on edit keeps it), and a fixed store REQUIRED when the role is an SPG role (any role holding `spg_sales:record`, `lib/users/validasi.ts`, tested); for any other role `assignedStoreId` is cleared. An admin cannot change their OWN role there (`PERAN_SENDIRI`). Every save writes `USER_CREATED`/`USER_UPDATED` to `AuditLog` in the same transaction — never the password, only `sandiDiganti`. Deactivating or deleting a user is NOT built. The seeded SPG account has a PIN but no password; set one here before it can log in on a fresh database.
- [x] SPG home rebuilt (2026-09-29): own bottom menu (Beranda, Jual, Stok, Nota, Lainnya — `lib/pwa/menu-spg.ts`, tested), check-in card with days worked this month, task list (deliveries waiting, stock running low), month vs target, 30-day best sellers in the store, today's receipts, pay & commission. All times formatted on the server (`app/pwa/page.tsx`), fixing the contrast/heading/hard-coded issues.
- [ ] **Store stock book for SPG stores** (owner decisions 2026-09-29, spec `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-29-dasbor-spg-stok-toko-design.md`). `StoreStockMovement` (TERIMA/JUAL/OPNAME, signed qty) — balance = Σ qty, computed, never stored, and **allowed to go negative**: an SPG sale is never refused for a zero book balance, an opname settles it. Stock ENTERS only when the SPG counts and confirms a delivery (`StoreReceipt`, unique per source document; sources: putus `FieldSalesDelivery`, approved KONSI `FieldSalesOrder`, `ConsignmentShipment`) or through a store opname (`StoreOpname`; the first one is the opening stock). `recordSpgSale` writes one JUAL per line in its own transaction, so an idempotent replay cuts nothing twice. "Waiting deliveries" start at the store's first movement, or the last 30 days before tracking starts — older shipments are already on the shelf and belong to an opname. Receipt lines without an `itemId` (typed-name consignment lines) are recorded but move no stock. Also `StockRequest` (SPG asks for stock, office moves it BARU→DIPROSES→SELESAI/DITOLAK under `stores:manage`) and `LostSale` (items shoppers asked for). Migrations: Postgres `15_stok_toko` (applied locally) + MariaDB `20260929140000_store_stock` (never run). A Store or Item with movements can no longer be deleted (Restrict) — deactivate instead.
- [ ] SPG sale no longer asks for cash: the store cashier takes the money (owner). `SpgSale.cashReceived`/`changeGiven` are still filled (= total, 0) for old reports. The SPG receipt prints "Bayar di kasir toko" instead of TUNAI/KEMBALI; only an old sale that recorded real change still prints the cash lines (`lib/print/spg-sale-nota-html.ts`, now WIB-pinned).
- [ ] SPG stock screens (`/pwa/spg/stok`, `/stok/minta`, `/terima`, `/opname`) were verified end to end on 2026-09-29 against the local database (receive with a shortfall, scan-sell, count, request, office processing, lost sale, store stock card). An opname in progress IS saved as a draft since 2026-10-01 (`localStorage["elorae.spg.opname.<storeId>"]`, dropped after 3 days, cleared on save, "Draf dipulihkan" card); the sale cart likewise survives leaving the screen (`sessionStorage["elorae.spg.keranjang.<storeId>"]`, with its idempotency key so a retry cannot record a second receipt). Scanning a parent SKU closes the camera so the size sheet is visible (`PemindaiBarcode` `onKode` returns `"tutup"`).
- [ ] `crypto.randomUUID` only exists in a secure context (https or localhost): a phone opening a LAN http address gets a form that throws. The SPG sale and stock-request forms use `newLocalId()` (`lib/pwa/id-lokal.ts`, with a fallback); `SmartRequestShell` and `DeliveryFormDialog` still call `crypto.randomUUID` directly (`VanSellShell` moved to `newLocalId` in phase 4). Prod is https, so this only bites on-device testing over LAN.
- [ ] Check-in when location permission is DENIED shows a static red notice with no next step (`app/pwa/stores/[id]/CheckInButton.tsx`, shared by salesman and SPG) — it should say how to re-enable location for the browser, and re-check when the page regains focus.
- [ ] PWA copy addresses the user as "Anda" (the SPG screens briefly used "kamu"; unified 2026-09-29). New PWA strings should follow.
- [ ] Barcode label content follows the owner's existing label since 2026-10-03 (`/backoffice/items/labels`, style `ELORAE`, no price); STKCOD value and paper size still open. The scanner (`components/spg/PemindaiBarcode.tsx`) matches variant barcode → variant SKU → item SKU (`cocokkanKode`, tested). It also takes a Bluetooth/USB scanner or a typed code + Enter.
- [ ] Camera scanning works in ANY browser that can open a camera (2026-09-29, owner asked for every phone): `lib/barcode/detektor.ts` uses the built-in `BarcodeDetector` when it supports at least one of our formats (Chrome on Android/Mac), otherwise the `barcode-detector` ponyfill (ZXing compiled to WASM) with the same API — Safari on iPhone/Mac, Firefox, Chrome on Windows, and Android without Google services. The ~1 MB WASM is SELF-HOSTED at `apps/web/public/vendor/zxing-wasm-<version>/zxing_reader.wasm` (committed, like `tessdata/`), never the library's default jsDelivr URL, and `/vendor/` is on the `proxy.ts` allow-list. **After upgrading `barcode-detector`, run `node scripts/salin-zxing-wasm.mjs` from `apps/web`** — the JS glue and the WASM must be the same version, and `lib/barcode/wasm-zxing.test.ts` fails (sha256 vs `ZXING_WASM_SHA256`) when the copy is stale. It is downloaded only the first time the camera opens in a browser without the built-in reader. Verified in the browser with the built-in reader removed and a simulated camera showing a Code 128 label (read in under a second, engine fetched from `/vendor/`, no outside request); NOT yet on a physical iPhone. The camera needs https (or localhost) — a phone opening a LAN http address gets no camera button at all.
- [ ] SPG pay & commission: official figures come from `ConsignmentCommission`; the running estimate (units sold × `Item.commissionPerPcs`) is computed for KONSI stores (split equally) and, since 2026-10-03, for PUTUS stores per SPG's own pieces (`spg.komisiPutus`, official rows in `SpgCommission`). Lost-goods charges are deducted from both.
- [ ] Consignment SPG report can be filled from the SPG's daily sales (`bahanLaporanSpg`, read-only; the admin reviews and saves through the existing `catatLaporan`). It aggregates per ITEM (variants merged), month in WIB.
- [ ] PWA i18n debt: hardcoded ID strings in `CatalogShell.tsx:180,192`, `VisitPhotoCapture.tsx:139-190`, lightbox `StoreDetailView.tsx:415,439` (pending-orders view already i18n'd) (#21).
- [ ] Extract a shared `QtyStepper` (phase 5 built `app/pwa/stores/[id]/catalog/QtyStepper.tsx` for the catalog, tinjau and variant sheet; van and SPG screens still have their own inline steppers, and it is not yet under `components/`) + finish `lib/sales-orders/format.ts` adoption (~9 sites still inline `Intl.NumberFormat`, plus seven copies of `formatRupiah` across the three finance report clients, `ReportSection`, and the three finance print pages — the print copies are deliberately isolated, the client ones are not); cross-page nav pending-state; CI `tsc --noEmit` gate (#21).
- [x] SPG: `User.assignedStoreId` is set from the store picker on Settings → Pengguna (#208).
- [x] SPG: offline write-queue for sales, receipts and counts, counter promos on SPG sales, and the store book vs consignment report reconciliation — all built 2026-10-03 (see "SPG tahap 2").
- [ ] Pack-ratio: the Settings card client re-implements `validateRows` instead of importing the client-safe `validatePackRatio` from `@elorae/db/pack-ratio` — exact parity today (server backstops), but a thin wrapper would kill the drift risk (#210).
- [ ] Pack-ratio: `settings_pack_ratio:view` defined but unreferenced (`getPackRatio` un-gated, matching the `getPpnRatePercent` sibling convention) — wire it if the page ever needs a read gate (#210).
- [x] Smart-request: `buildSmartRequestAction` was auth-only — the 2026-10-01 "Rencana kirim" actions refuse SPG, inactive stores and (with the own-store lock on) other people's stores. Building a draft without a check-in is now deliberate: the screen says so and only sending needs the visit.
- [x] Smart-request KONSI guard — obsolete: "Rencana kirim" supports KONSI on purpose (2026-10-01), with price-free KONSI drafts proven by JSON tests.
- [ ] Rencana kirim: no promo/bonus preview in the draft (PUTUS shows a gross estimate; the writer still applies promos); online-only (no offline queue); the draft reserves nothing, so warehouse stock can change before sending (the writer re-checks).
- [ ] Smart-request: a selected category with zero candidate articles yields `underfill>0, lines=0, dropped=0` → renders the generic "tidak ada produk" empty state without the underfill note (cosmetic; same outcome conveyed) (feat/smart-request).
- [ ] Konsi suggestions: candidates with zero or NEGATIVE availability are listed with no filter and no sort — every never-sent variant appears, out-of-stock ones only distinguished by a disabled stepper, and the list is sorted by SKU alone. On a large catalog the admin scrolls past unusable rows to reach the sellable ones. Wants an availability sort (or a "hide out of stock" toggle), which is also the cheapest fix for the negative-availability row printing a bare `-3` (feat/konsi-never-sent-suggestions).
- [ ] Konsi suggestions: an added item whose `Item.sellingPrice` is `null` prices at 0 through `computeStorePrice`, so it transfers at zero value with no warning anywhere — not in the panel, not in the approve dialog, not on the order detail. The PWA catalog already has the idiom for this ("Harga belum diset" tag on an in-stock unpriced item); the panel should carry the same tag, and arguably refuse to stage it (feat/konsi-never-sent-suggestions).
- [ ] Konsi suggestions: `apps/web/app/backoffice/field-sales-orders/[id]/loading.tsx` is hardcoded English on a page that is otherwise fully translated, so an `id`-locale admin sees an English skeleton flash (feat/konsi-never-sent-suggestions).
- [ ] Konsi suggestions: `konsi-added-lines.test.ts` leaks two `AdminNotification` rows per test (~30 per run, and it grows with every case added) — `createFieldSalesOrder` writes one per seeded order and the teardown never deletes them (the fan-out itself is mocked, so nothing reaches `NotificationQueue`). Harmless but it accumulates on the shared `:3308` bed, and the bell's hard 50-row window is already a known problem. Delete them in `afterEach` scoped to the seeded order ids (feat/konsi-never-sent-suggestions).
- [ ] Konsi suggestions: the `onOrder` exclusion in `listKonsiSuggestions` is dead code — `sentItemIds` covers non-REJECTED konsi lines, which includes the PENDING_APPROVAL order being approved, so every item on the order is already excluded by `sent`. The writer's own `onOrder` check is NOT dead (it must fire before `alreadySent` to return `DUPLICATE` rather than `ALREADY_SENT`), but the query's is, and its test cannot distinguish the two paths. Either drop it or add a case only it can satisfy (feat/konsi-never-sent-suggestions).
- [ ] Delivery: `consumeFieldSalesOrder` (whole-order) has NO production caller left — the delivery path uses `consumeFieldSalesOrderPartial`. Anyone reaching for the old one bypasses the delivery document entirely (no `FieldSalesDelivery` row, no per-delivery `SalesHistory`, `deliveredQty` never moves). Either delete it or rename it so the trap is visible (feat/field-sales-delivery).
- [ ] Delivery: `StockReservation.consumedQty` means two different things by source. The field-sales partial consume maintains it; `consumeOrder` (Jubelio) never sets it, so a JUBELIO row reads `consumedQty = 0` while `state = 'CONSUMED'`. Any future reader must branch on `source` or it will conclude Jubelio reservations were never consumed (feat/field-sales-delivery).
- [ ] Delivery: `onHandByKey` in `getFieldSalesOrderById` SUMS the `variantSku` null and `""` inventory rows for a key, while `findFieldSalesInventory` (the writer's lookup) picks the FIRST of them. On an item that somehow has both rows, the delivery form's on-hand cap is larger than the row the consume will actually decrement — the form offers a quantity the writer then refuses with `INSUFFICIENT_STOCK`. **The PR #229 review bot rated this a live form↔writer contract bug rather than a theoretical dual-row edge case, and it is right**: the two paths disagree by construction, not only when data is malformed. Fix by aligning the read path with the writer (same first-row rule), or by merging the dual rows before any cap is exposed (feat/field-sales-delivery, PR #229). **It now has a SECOND consumer:** the konsi never-sent panel auto-expands off `shortLineCount`, which is derived from the same summed `available`, while `listKonsiSuggestions` deliberately takes the MINIMUM of a dual row — so on a dual-row item the panel can stay collapsed for a line the writer will refuse, and the admin is given no hint that the suggestions are the remedy. Fixing the read path fixes both surfaces at once (feat/konsi-never-sent-suggestions).
- [ ] Delivery: the `INSUFFICIENT_STOCK` error reports the on-hand it read MID-TRANSACTION, after earlier lines in the same call already decremented. The transaction then rolls back, so the number the operator is shown (and that the form snaps the input down to) is not the on-hand that exists afterwards — it is lower. Harmless understatement, but the toast is stating a figure that is never true (feat/field-sales-delivery).
- [ ] Delivery: `consumeFieldSalesOrderPartial` filters reservations by `fieldSalesLineId` + `state` but NOT by `source: "FIELD_SALES"`, unlike `releaseFieldSalesOrder` beside it. Nothing else writes `fieldSalesLineId` today so it is unreachable, but the sibling's defence-in-depth is missing (feat/field-sales-delivery).
- [ ] Delivery: `logPrint` is called with the order-era entity types `"FieldSalesNotaGudang"`/`"FieldSalesNotaTagihan"` but now passed a DELIVERY id, so the audit log's entity ids silently changed meaning for those two types. Any historical row predates the change and points at an order (feat/field-sales-delivery).
- [ ] Delivery: the nota tagihan is built with `appliedPromoName`/`appliedOrderPromoName` hardcoded `null`, so a discounted delivery prints the discount figure with no promo name beside it — unlike the order detail, which names it (feat/field-sales-delivery).
- [ ] Delivery: `deliveryStatus` is absent from the field-sales-orders LIST page — no column, no filter — so finding the orders still awaiting delivery means opening them one at a time. The detail page is the only surface that shows it (feat/field-sales-delivery).
- [ ] Delivery: the price-appeal → `SalesHistory` path lost its only cover when this branch deleted the approve-time history assertions; nothing now asserts that an owner-set final price reaches `SalesHistory` through the delivery path (feat/field-sales-delivery).
- [ ] `deleteItem`'s link checks never look at `FieldSalesOrderLine` or `FieldSalesDeliveryLine`, so an item referenced only by field-sales documents passes the "safe to delete" check and the delete then fails on the real FK (or, for the `relationMode = "prisma"` relations, succeeds and dangles) (feat/field-sales-delivery).
- [ ] Delivery: `getStoreOrderSummary` still reports `FieldSalesOrder.total` on the store profile and the PWA "Pesanan terakhir", which overstates a partly-delivered or remainder-closed order — nothing recomputes the order total. `getStoreSentItems` and the dashboard realised bucket were fixed; this one was left (feat/field-sales-delivery).
- [x] (2026-10-06: dashboard bucket "Disetujui, belum dikirim", link `?kirim=PENDING&orderType=PUTUS`) **Delivery — highest-priority of this batch:** an APPROVED putus order awaiting its first delivery is in NEITHER dashboard bucket. Realised counts deliveries, outstanding counts orders awaiting approval, so **every newly approved putus order disappears from both cards until someone delivers it** — which reads as data loss to ops, on day one of the feature. Either widen outstanding to "ordered but not yet fully delivered or closed", or add an explicit third bucket (feat/field-sales-delivery, PR #229).
- [ ] Delivery: `recordDeliveryAction` returns `OVER_DELIVER` for a malformed payload — a non-string `orderLineId`, a non-integer or non-positive `qty` — so a client/shape error surfaces to the operator as "Delivered quantity exceeds the outstanding quantity", which is simply untrue and misleads anyone debugging it. `closeRemainderAction` does the same with an empty `reason`, returning `INVALID_STATE` ("cannot be delivered in its current state"). Both should return `INVALID_REQUEST`, which already exists on `DeliveryActionResult` and already has strings in both locales (feat/field-sales-delivery, PR #229).
- [ ] Delivery: `recordFieldSalesDelivery` maps every non-`INSUFFICIENT_STOCK` `PartialConsumeError` — notably `OVER_CONSUME` — onto `OVER_DELIVER`. That collapses the one diagnosis that matters for the deploy-window case: an order approved by the OLD image after the backfill migration has already run is stock-consumed with its reservation `CONSUMED`, receives no `bf_` delivery, and then reads as `PENDING` with full outstanding under the new app. Delivering it hits `OVER_CONSUME`, and the operator is told "quantity exceeds outstanding" with no hint that the order is stuck behind a deploy race. Surface a distinct reason so the remedy (hand-run backfill statements 2–6 scoped to that order id) is discoverable (feat/field-sales-delivery, PR #229).
- [ ] Delivery: the doc comment on `allocateDeliveryDiscounts` (`lib/field-sales/delivery/plan.ts`) says stranding needs three chunks on one line plus a fourth delivery. That holds only for an INTEGER discount — the promo engine does not round (`PERCENT` is a bare `(lineTotal * value) / 100` into a `Decimal(15,2)`), so a fractional `discountAmount` strands its sub-rupiah part on a TWO-delivery order whenever the discounted line finishes on the non-closing one. The fold absorbs it correctly and the totals stay exact; only the comment understates the trigger, and a cheaper regression test exists than the four-delivery one (feat/field-sales-delivery).
- [ ] Delivery: the closing delivery's `total` can in principle go negative, because it absorbs every stranded remainder plus the order-discount remainder while carrying only its own goods. Bounded by roughly 0.5 × chunks-per-stranded-line plus fractional parts, so it only bites when the closing delivery is worth a rupiah or two — unreachable at realistic prices, but it would print a negative grand total on the nota tagihan and push a negative `SalesHistory.orderTotal` (feat/field-sales-delivery).
- [ ] Delivery: `getStoreSentItems`'s delivered branch has no `orderType` filter. Safe today because `recordFieldSalesDelivery` refuses non-PUTUS, so only putus orders can carry deliveries — but if konsi ever gains a delivery document, a konsi order would be counted twice, once through its delivery and once through the approved-konsi union beside it (feat/field-sales-delivery).
- [ ] Nota dates are entered by the operator and are no longer derived from `Store.paymentTempo` (the client's locked answer to the order-processing due-date question). `computeDueDate` survives as a client-side suggestion behind the "Pakai tempo" button. **Deliveries recorded before this carry derived due dates and are not marked as such** — nothing distinguishes them from entered ones, so a due date on an older nota may not be what anyone chose. They are correctable one at a time from the deliveries card.
- [ ] `updateDeliveryDatesAction` corrects an issued nota's dates with a required reason and an `AuditLog` row written in the same transaction. There is no UI that surfaces those audit rows next to the delivery — they are only visible through the general audit log viewer.
- [ ] The delivery dialog's `parseDateOnlyInput` duplicates `parseDateOnly`'s WIB-midnight parsing because the shared helper lives in a module the client can import but returns `undefined` rather than `null` and accepts looser input. Worth reconciling into one client-safe helper rather than two spellings of the same rule. Both were traced during the manual-nota-dates review: the client's accepted set is a strict SUBSET of the server's, so there is no behavioural divergence today — it is duplication, not a defect. The helper is now exported from `DeliveryFormDialog` and reused by the edit dialog, so there are two spellings, not three.
- [ ] Nota dates: a delivery retry after a lost response replays the `idempotencyKey` and returns the ORIGINAL delivery, discarding any dates the operator corrected between attempts, while reporting success. The key deliberately survives a failed submit so a genuine retry cannot double-ship, so the fix is a semantics decision — either detect a date mismatch on replay and return a distinct reason, or rotate the key whenever a date input changes — not a patch.
- [ ] Nota dates: the deliveries table shows the due date but never the invoice date, so after a correction an operator can verify only half of what they changed without reopening the dialog or printing. An eighth column would hurt the mobile layout; an `invoice → due` pair inside the existing column is the likelier answer.
- [ ] Nota dates: pressing Esc during a pending save, then opening the edit dialog on another row, lets the resolving transition's `setEditOpen(false)` snap the new dialog shut. Same pattern as the pre-existing create dialog, so fixing it means fixing both.
- [ ] `fmtDocDate`/`fmtDocDateTime` in `apps/web/lib/print/print-theme.ts` are now WIB-pinned (`timeZone: "Asia/Jakarta"`). They were formatting in the ambient zone, which is the BROWSER's — every print builder is called from a click handler in a `"use client"` file — so a WIB-midnight instant printed one calendar day early on any machine west of WIB. **On a WIB machine this changed nothing at all**; off WIB it corrected six other documents besides the nota tagihan (van load, van reconcile, konsi surat keluar, PO, material issue, PO payment receipt, plus the inventory and vendor-return timestamps). It would also have been wrong unconditionally had any of those ever been built server-side, since prod runs UTC. Remaining sibling of the same bug class: `toDisplayDate` in `lib/date-only.ts` (already listed under Sales UI / dates).
- [ ] `apps/web/app/actions/field-sales-deliveries.spec.ts` was named `.test.ts` when it landed, and `apps/web/vitest.config.ts` includes `lib/**/*.test.ts` but only `app/**/*.spec.ts` — so it had never executed, not even when named explicitly on the command line (a positional arg filters the include set, it does not widen it). Renamed to `.spec.ts` to match every other `app/` spec. **Anything under `app/` named `*.test.ts` is silently dead**; there is no guard against adding another one, and no CI job runs the suite at all.
- [ ] `updateDeliveryDatesAction`'s `CONFLICT` result is effectively unreachable in production. The `before` snapshot is read INSIDE the serializable transaction that then compare-and-swaps, and MySQL SERIALIZABLE promotes that read to a shared lock, so no writer can move the row in between — a competing correction blocks or deadlocks and is retried with a fresh read, after which the swap hits. The compare-and-swap is legitimate defence-in-depth and the audit `before` is now always truthful, but the operator-facing "someone else just changed this" toast will not fire in practice. The lost update an operator CAN still hit is the stale-UI one: B's dialog was seeded from server-rendered props showing X, A commits Y, B saves Z — B's transaction reads Y fresh, the swap hits, and Z wins silently with a truthful `before: Y`. Closing that would mean the dialog sending the pair it was seeded with as an optimistic token.
- [ ] The `CONFLICT` spec case drives a mocked transaction client that writes outside a real transaction, so it proves no audit row is written on a compare-and-swap miss but does not prove transactional rollback. Rollback was confirmed structurally instead — both early returns precede the audit create inside the same callback. The test is not weaker than it claims, just narrower than it looks.
- [ ] `apps/web/lib/print/print-theme.ts` is now mixed-quote: the two timezone-fixed formatters use double quotes per the house rule, while `esc` and `money` beside them still use single. Consistent where touched, inconsistent within the file.
- [ ] Faktur pajak: `recordNotaTagihanPrinted` is best-effort end to end — a failed audit write, a failed notification create or a rejected fan-out is caught and `console.error`'d, and the action returns `void` either way, so the operator is never told and no durable flag records it. A nota can therefore print with finance never notified and nothing anywhere saying so; the only recovery is that the row is already in the queue by construction (it is created with the delivery, not by the print), which is why the queue rather than the ping is the durable signal. Same shape as the van journal actions' silent success, and it would want the same fix: return the failure and warn.
- [ ] Faktur pajak: `TaxInvoice.markedById` and `notaPrintedById` carry no `@@index`, unlike `status`. Nothing queries by them today — the queue filters on status and searches through the delivery — so this only bites the first "what did this user mark" report, or a `User` delete that has to scan for references under `relationMode = "prisma"`.
- [ ] Faktur pajak: the print click blocks on FIVE sequential server round trips (auth, the print audit write, the CAS, the delivery lookup, the notification create) before `printHtml` opens the preview, where it used to await one `logPrint`. The button is disabled through the transition so a double-click can't double-fire, but the operator waits on work that has nothing to do with the document being rendered. The notification half could be fired and forgotten after the CAS, or the whole call moved after the preview opens.
- [ ] Faktur pajak: the nota tagihan print dialog's confirm button is disabled on `isPending` but keeps its normal label, unlike every sibling dialog on the same card (close-remainder, update-dates) and the faktur queue's own dialogs, which all swap to a "submitting…" label. With five round trips behind it (item above) that silent gap is the longest on the page.

### Complete salesman app (2026-09-30 → 10-01)
- [ ] **Nothing is committed, and the full `apps/web` type-check has never run over this work** — only per-file scoped checks. The Docker build in the deploy workflow is the first place a cross-file type error would surface (see the DocType note under Code conventions). Run it before opening the PR.
- [ ] Store portal: a dead or revoked link returns HTTP 200, not 404, because the root `app/loading.tsx` streams the page before `notFound()` runs (the response is still `noindex`, generic, with no store info). A real 404 needs a check in `proxy.ts` or no root loading boundary for `/toko`.
- [ ] Store portal: the per-day order limit is counted outside the writer's transaction, so a burst of parallel submits can exceed it by a few.
- [ ] Store portal: price previews are not rate-limited, and both preview and submit refuse a quantity above available stock by item name — a link holder can binary-search exact stock. Either rate-limit per link or stop refusing on exact stock.
- [ ] Store portal: orders are credited to the store's holding salesman, so they count toward that person's sales, targets and commission estimate. Owner decision whether they should.
- [ ] Goods exchange: approval reserves no stock; completion re-checks and refuses with `STOK_KURANG`. Online-only (no offline queue). `TKR/<year>/NNNN` numbering breaks past 9 999 per year (text sort).
- [ ] Display checklist: a fresh database has NO template until the office presses "Salin template bawaan" — the default lives in code. Sending is online-only; template and guide edits write no audit log; reports read at most 5 000 rows; SPGs are not blocked from the pages.
- [ ] Competitor prices have no idempotency column — an identical entry from the same person at the same store within 10 minutes counts as one.
- [ ] Collection tasks: "Tandai sudah ditagih" is online-only.
- [ ] Daily route: distances are straight line × 1.3 (real road routing needs a paid map API); stores without a pin are listed but left out of the Google Maps links; `ruteHariIni` has no DB test.
- [ ] Proof of delivery: the salesman is not notified when the office records or corrects a proof; proof status is not shown on the order lists; the replay key lives only in `AuditLog` metadata and is matched in JS.
- [ ] Visit photos never upload in local dev (R2 is not configured → 503), so photo answers in the checklist stay "Belum terunggah" locally.
- [ ] Observed in dev on `/pwa/stores/[id]`: after streaming, a hidden copy of the page stays in `div#S:0` (height 0, `hidden`), so every control exists twice in the DOM. Invisible and not caused by this work, but duplicate element ids and test selectors (`find` returns two matches) are a trap.
- [ ] The backoffice user menu ("My Account", "Theme", "Sign out") is hardcoded English while the rest of the shell is translated.
- [ ] Pre-order: planned qty does not cap orders; windows apply to every PUTUS store (no per-store scoping); salesmen are not notified at release; approved-but-unreleased pre-orders count in the "approved / ready to ship" tabs. Bonus promos on a pre-order are computed from READY stock, so a bonus that is itself the pre-order item is noted as short.
- [ ] **DB specs that create field-sales orders leak `AdminNotification` rows, far beyond the one konsi spec listed under Field Sales.** `createFieldSalesOrder` writes a `PENDING_ORDER_APPROVAL` row (and sometimes `STOCK_OVERSELL_RISK`) that no teardown deletes. One combined run over the changed areas on 2026-10-01 left **118** orphaned `PENDING_ORDER_APPROVAL` + 1 `STOCK_OVERSELL_RISK` rows (deleted by hand: rows whose `metadata.orderId` no longer exists). A shared teardown helper that deletes notifications by `metadata.orderId` of the spec's own orders would close it for every spec at once — `tukar-writer.test.ts` shows the JS-side match (JSON-path filtering is unreliable across adaptors).
- [x] Salesman-app gaps found by the 2026-10-01 audit — cancel/edit own pending order, WhatsApp order copy, office visit schedule, announcements, leave requests, per-category/product targets, store notes — all built the same day (stage 6, see "Complete salesman app").
- [ ] Stage 6: the office's `PENDING_ORDER_APPROVAL` bell item for an order the salesman later cancelled or replaced stays unread and opens the cancelled order (nothing writes `AdminNotification.readAt`).
- [ ] Stage 6: editing a smart-request order through the catalog re-applies the minimum (`skipMinQty` is not carried); an edited PORTAL order stays PORTAL, so only the holder rule judges the edit.
- [ ] Stage 6 leave: clock-in on an approved leave day is not blocked on the server (the card only offers a secondary "Tetap absen masuk"); Sunday is a fixed non-working day, so an SPG who works Sundays cannot request one — owner question; public holidays are not handled; leave requests are online-only.
- [ ] Stage 6 announcements: the read report lists CURRENT field users of the audience, so someone added later shows as "belum membaca" without ever having been pushed.
- [ ] Stage 6 targets: category = the item's category TODAY, not at sale time; bonus lines count 0 pcs; the settings page's "Capaian" column has no end-of-today cap while home and targets do.
- [ ] Stage 6 schedule: switching the own-store lock on after scheduling leaves rows the salesperson can no longer check into; the Excel export and the 18:30 daily summary do not include schedule compliance.
- [ ] Salesman-app offline coverage: orders, visit photos, BKM, store returns, payment promises and proof of delivery queue on the phone; check-in/out, travel expenses, attendance, goods exchange, checklist, competitor prices, new-store proposals and "sudah ditagih" all need signal. Check-in is the one that matters most — without signal a salesman cannot start a visit, so cannot take an order through the visit flow.
- [ ] Invoices with NO seller (imports) at a store with NO holder are in no salesman's bills list (`daftarPiutang({ penagihId })`) while the store summary still counts them — locally Toko Sinar Jaya showed 14,2 jt on Tagihan vs 18,45 jt on its summary. Assigning holders fixes the data; an office view of "invoices nobody collects" would make it visible.
- [x] The bills list counted already-settled invoices in "N faktur" (`tokoLapangan`); it now counts only invoices with a balance, and a fully settled store keeps its total count.
- [x] The attendance history labelled every day "Tidak absen" while attendance was switched OFF; it now shows "—" until the office enables it.
- [x] `app/pwa/stores/[id]/kirim-katalog/loading.tsx` used `getTranslations` from `next-intl/server`, but the app has NO server-side next-intl config (locale is client-only), so the loading screen would throw. Now a client component with `useTranslations`. Never import `next-intl/server` in this app.

### SPG lengkap / dashboard (2026-10-01)
- [ ] Nothing committed; the full `apps/web` type-check has not run over this work (scoped checks only). The MariaDB migration `20261001120000_spg_transfer_returns` has never run.
- [x] Store transfers queue offline too (request + mark shipped, 2026-10-03); cancelling a transfer still needs signal.
- [ ] SPG shipping a transfer in a locked consignment month is refused with no office path to ship (the office can only cancel).
- [x] Damaged (RUSAK) shopper returns write no book row, so `stok-di-toko` sales speed still counts them as sold — every "laku" reader now goes through `lib/spg/laku-bersih.ts` (2026-10-07).
- [x] (phase 5: `?status=`/`?koreksi=1`, and "Tertahan retur" links to `/backoffice/piutang?keadaan=tertahan`) `/backoffice/pelunasan` has no URL status filter, so the dashboard "BKM menunggu verifikasi" row opens the unfiltered list; "Tertahan retur" still links to `/retur-toko` unfiltered.
- [ ] `lib/dashboard/aktivitas.ts` (+ test) is unused since the audit feed left the dashboard.
- [ ] Store analysis says "belum punya kiriman" for a store whose deliveries have no lines (only old local test data does).
- [ ] Cron markers for transfer reminders are `AuditLog` rows attributed to the creator / first SPG (there is no system user).
- [ ] Shopper returns are online-only and need no check-in; a return counts in the RECEIPT's month for targets but in the RETURN's month for commission/consignment reports (locked months never change) — owner may want one rule.

See the Prisma `updateMany` nuance above.
