### Notifications
- [x] The three web `AdminNotification` categories now reach the in-app bell. `fanOutAdminNotification` (`lib/notifications/admin-fanout.ts`) runs after each flag write and fans out `NotificationQueue` rows to the users holding the permission that can ACT on the category — `journals:manage`, `field_sales_orders:approve`, `stores:manage` — reusing the same delivery helpers the four existing push types use. Best-effort by design: a failure costs a bell ping, not state.
- [ ] **`AdminNotification` is NOT write-only, and must not be deleted.** It is a durable per-document state flag, and `lib/canvassing/journal-pending.ts` reads it both to gate the "Post journal" retry button on four screens AND to authorize `postVanLoadJournalAction` / `postVanSaleJournalAction` / `postVanReconcileJournalAction` — every export of a `"use server"` module is an independently callable endpoint. `NotificationQueue` is per-user delivery; conflating the two would make a retry button's authorization depend on whether somebody read their notification. The eventual clean split is to move the journal-pending flag to a purpose-built column or table, after which the model really can go.
- [ ] `apps/api`'s `AdminNotificationService.write` still writes flag rows that reach nobody. The api has no Firebase admin and `NotificationQueue` delivery is web-owned, so wiring it needs a cross-service decision.
- [ ] `app/actions/notifications.ts` is `'use server'` with ~18 exports and no `auth()` or `hasPermission` check in the file. No client component imports it, so the action ids do not ship in a browser bundle and this is a hardening gap rather than a live hole — but Next registers every export as a callable action. The two delivery helpers were moved to `lib/notifications/recipients.ts` to get them out of the registry; the rest were left alone.
- [x] Prod RBAC precondition for the fan-out VERIFIED 2026-08-13: `journals:manage`, `field_sales_orders:approve` and `stores:manage` all exist as `Permission` rows on prod, so all three categories resolve recipients normally. Prod resolves **1 recipient** for every category — a single ADMIN user, via the `isSystem` branch. Note dev is MISSING `journals:manage` (only `packages/db/prisma/seed.ts` defines it and the deploy pipeline never seeds), which is what the `getUsersWithPermission` missing-row fix covers — dev still resolved its admin.
- [ ] **Backfilling the `JOURNAL_PENDING` backlog is measured and DEFERRED — it is NOT a routine post-merge step.** The script is `pnpm exec tsx scripts/backfill-journal-pending-notifications.ts` from `apps/web` (dry-run first, then `--apply --confirm-prod-writes`); idempotent, dedups on the `AdminNotification` row id. Dry-run against prod 2026-08-13: it would create exactly **50 rows for 1 recipient**. All 50 targets were written in a single 4-second window on `2026-07-26T04:00:01–04:00:03`, every one `"Sales journal not posted"` with `reason: UNMAPPED_ROLE` (`AR`/`SALES_REVENUE`/`COGS`) — one sweep-stall batch, not 50 distinct incidents. That falsifies the spec's D6 rationale ("these stay actionable indefinitely, someone must press the retry button"): nobody can act on them while the GL cutover stays unarmed, and once it IS armed `postPendingSalesJournals` re-posts them with no button pressed, so the flags go stale on their own. Applying it today takes the bell from 7 rows to 57 — past the hard `LIMIT = 50` — evicting all 7 real notifications, moving the badge from 5 to 55, with no mark-all-read for the single admin to clear it and no document number in any of the 50 otherwise-identical messages. Revisit after mark-all-read ships, or drop it.
- [x] (phase 5: all three link through; `JOURNAL_PENDING` per metadata shape, office-side kabar types link to their office pages; field-only kabar stay link-less in the office bell) Bell items for the three newly-delivered categories navigate nowhere. `getNotificationHref` (`lib/notifications/navigation.ts`) has cases for ~18 existing types but none for `JOURNAL_PENDING`, `PENDING_ORDER_APPROVAL` or `STORE_CHANGE_REQUEST`, and returns `null` for anything unmapped — so those items render and mark-as-read on click, but do not link through. `PENDING_ORDER_APPROVAL` (`orderId`) and `STORE_CHANGE_REQUEST` (`storeId`) are one-line cases; `JOURNAL_PENDING` is genuinely varied (`docId`+`kind`, `orderId`+`kind`, `opnameId`, `salesReturnId`, `receiptId`+`woId`) and needs a per-kind mapping. Note the one-shot backfill wrote `data` containing only `adminNotificationId`, so backfilled rows would lack any metadata field a future case relies on.
- [x] (phase 5: cursor pagination `(createdAt, id)` + "Muat lebih", `unreadCount` counts every unread row, `POST /api/notifications/read-all` marks all — still NO retention window, nothing prunes `NotificationQueue`) **The bell can permanently fill up, and on prod it will.** `app/api/notifications/route.ts` has a hard `LIMIT = 50` with no cursor and no load-more in `NotificationIcon`; the only way to set `readAt` is clicking an individual item (no mark-all-read); `unreadCount` counts **all** unread rather than the visible 50; and nothing anywhere prunes `NotificationQueue`. So once an admin passes 50 unread rows, everything past position 50 is unreachable from any surface and the badge can never return to zero. The ceiling is real and unfixed, but the URGENCY was measured on prod 2026-08-13 and is far lower than this item used to claim. Prod has **1 admin, not three**; `JOURNAL_PENDING` is **0/day** over the last 7 days (50 total, all one batch on 2026-07-26); `PENDING_ORDER_APPROVAL` is **1 row ever** (2026-07-06); `STORE_CHANGE_REQUEST` is 0; and `NotificationQueue` holds 7 rows. So the live fan-out is not filling anything today, and the van-sale flood this item used to predict is contingent on canvassing entering real prod use while `CASH` is still unmapped — which has not happened. **The superseded estimate ("twenty sales a day across three admins, sixty rows a day") was extrapolated from the dev bed, which is the opposite shape** — dev runs 22.5/day `PENDING_ORDER_APPROVAL` and sits at 45 unread of 52, because it absorbs all the field-sales testing. Never size prod notification volume from `:3308`. Cheapest useful pair remains a mark-all-read endpoint plus either pagination or a retention window; mark-all-read is the one that unblocks the deferred backfill above.
- [ ] Several `JOURNAL_PENDING` messages name no document. `app/actions/grn.ts` says "post from the GRN row" with no `docNumber` anywhere in the title, message or metadata — only `grnId`. Same for FG receipt, sales return and supplier payment. Before the fan-out nobody read those strings; now they are the entire user-facing surface, so a bell item saying "a GRN journal failed" with no document number and no link is delivered but not actionable. Fixing it means changing the `AdminNotification` title/message at four sites, which the fan-out branch deliberately forbade itself from touching.
- [ ] Best-effort delivery is safe "because another surface still tells someone" for six of the eight `JOURNAL_PENDING` sources and for both other categories, but that argument **fails for the supplier-payment `payment` direction**: its retry hint is "unmark payment on the PO and mark it paid again", and a PO left marked paid with no payment journal is already recorded above as invisible after a page reload. The bell is that alert's first and only rendering, so its delivery is genuinely load-bearing rather than redundant.
- [ ] A fourth `AdminNotification` category exists and stays bell-invisible: `STOCK_OVERSELL_RISK`, written from `packages/db/src/reservation-writer.ts` on both the Jubelio reserve path and the field-sales reserve path. Out of scope for the same reason as the api-side writer — `packages/db` has no Firebase and wiring it needs the cross-service decision — but the fan-out spec says "three categories", which reads as full web coverage.
- [ ] The backfill's documented apply command passes `--confirm-prod-writes`, which is the single flag that disables every branch of its port guard. Following the documentation degrades the prod check to a `console.warn`; the only real gate is `--apply`.
- [ ] The backfill selects on `readAt: null`. Since phase 4 `readAt` on a `JOURNAL_PENDING` means "its journal has since posted" (`tandaiJurnalTertundaSelesai`), so the filter now skips resolved flags; older rows written before phase 4 still have `readAt` null whether or not they were fixed — no date floor, no cap. Its dry run does print the counts before any write.
- [ ] Running the backfill while the live path is also writing can duplicate a bell row for the same alert: the backlog read and the already-delivered read are not point-in-time with each other. Run it during a quiet window.
- [ ] `getUsersWithPermission`'s missing-permission-row branch (`lib/notifications/recipients.ts`) has no test. It is the fix that makes the fan-out reach anyone on prod at all — without it a missing `Permission` row returned `[]` and silently excluded even system-role admins, who hold the `'*'` wildcard from `lib/auth.ts`. There is no `recipients.test.ts`, and `admin-fanout.test.ts` mocks `./recipients` wholesale, so nothing exercises it: someone who later "simplifies" the ternary back to an early return would see every spec still pass while the behaviour silently regresses. It IS testable — `getUsersWithPermission` is not behind the `VITEST` guard, so a DB spec could seed an `isSystem` role and call it with a permission code that has no row. That was deliberately not added here, because seeding roles and users against the shared `:3308` bed is the same hazard class this branch spent two fix rounds removing from other specs, and it would need teardown through `seededId()` to be safe.

### UI consistency (sweep of 2026-09-25)
- [ ] **No native date/time inputs** (2026-10-01, the owner rejected Safari's grey native picker). Every `<Input type="date|datetime-local|time">` (61 + 4 + 3, in 37 files) was replaced by `TanggalInput` / `TanggalJamInput` / `JamInput` from `components/ui/tanggal-input.tsx`, which take the SAME value strings ("YYYY-MM-DD", "YYYY-MM-DDTHH:mm", "HH:mm") and call `onChange` with `{ target: { value } }`, so existing handlers did not change. The calendar is the app's own (month/year dropdowns, Today, Clear when not `required`); on screens ≤640 px it opens as a bottom sheet, elsewhere as a popover. `name` renders a hidden input for plain form posts. Pure helpers in `lib/tanggal-isian.ts` (tested) map text ↔ LOCAL-midnight `Date` so the browser timezone never shifts the day; "today" is WIB. In the phone app the calendar cell size is 44 px (`globals.css`, `body:has(.pwa-app) [data-slot='calendar']`) — without it the 44 px touch rule inflates the day buttons and the rows overflow the calendar. An ESLint `no-restricted-syntax` ERROR now rejects `type="date|datetime-local|time|month|week"` in app/components. The older `DatePicker`/`DateRangePicker` are now thin `Date`-valued wrappers over `TanggalInput` / `RentangTanggalInput` (same local-midnight output as before), so every date control in the app looks and behaves the same.
- [ ] **A date RANGE is ONE `RentangTanggalInput`, never a "Dari tanggal" + "Sampai tanggal" pair** (2026-10-01, owner: "kenapa tidak bisa menjadi satu saja yang bisa saya langsung tarik?"). `components/ui/rentang-tanggal-input.tsx`: value `dari`/`sampai` strings, `onChange({ dari, sampai })` always ordered start ≤ end. Three ways to pick in one calendar: press and DRAG start→end (mouse or finger — the day under the finger is read with `elementFromPoint` from `data-tanggal`, because touch pointer events stay captured by the first button; the grid is `touch-none` so a drag never scrolls), tap start then tap end (hover previews), or a quick pick (today … this year, computed in WIB by `rentangCepat`). A finished range applies and closes at once — no "Terapkan". Options: `pilihanCepat={false}` for forward-looking forms, `required` (no Clear), `bolehTerbuka` (adds "Tanpa tanggal akhir" → `sampai: ""`), `maksHari` (days too far from the first pick are disabled), `nameDari`/`nameSampai` for plain form posts. Two months side by side at ≥900 px, one month in a bottom sheet on phones. Pages that update the URL must write BOTH params in ONE push (`pushParams({ from, to })`) — two single-key pushes race and the first is lost. Pairs that are NOT a range (invoice date vs due date, handover vs promised date, announcement publish vs expiry) stay as two `TanggalInput`s. **`components/ui/calendar.tsx`'s `Root`/`Chevron`/`WeekNumber` overrides must stay at module level**: defined inline they were a new component type every render, React remounted the whole day grid on each render, and a click whose pointerdown and pointerup straddled a re-render (the range hover preview) was silently lost.
- [ ] On-screen dates go through ONE helper: `formatDisplayDate` / `formatDisplayDateTime` in `apps/web/lib/date-only.ts` ("26 Agu 2026", WIB-pinned, en → en-GB). A bare `toLocaleDateString()` formats in the browser's zone and locale — it printed "8/26/2026, 12:00 AM" on an English browser. Machine formats (date inputs, URL params, exports, `app/print/`) are deliberately untouched.
- [ ] **A destructive confirm button is `<AlertDialogAction variant="destructive">`, never `className="bg-destructive …"`.** `AlertDialogAction` renders `Button asChild`, whose Slot concatenates classes without tailwind-merge, so `bg-primary` and `bg-destructive` both land and the lime primary won — every "Hapus" confirm in the app was green until 2026-10-01 (fixed in six files). Master-data pages (Jenis Pemasok, Kategori) now follow one shape: full width (the owner rejected a capped `max-w-4xl` block as "terpotong"; only the actions column has a fixed width so the rest share the space instead of leaving one hole beside Nama), the add button in the page header, explicit edit + delete icon buttons (never a pencil that opens a menu), a "Dipakai" usage column, delete disabled with its reason when in use, pagination only when there is a second page, and write actions returning `{ ok:false, code }` (`DUPLICATE_CODE`/`IN_USE`) instead of throwing.
- [ ] Audit-log codes are never shown raw: `auditActionLabel` / `auditEntityLabel` in `apps/web/lib/audit/action-label.ts` (namespace `auditActions`, humanised fallback for unknown codes). A new audit action wants a key there.
- [ ] **Tailwind's `xl` starts at exactly 1280 px**, so `hidden xl:table-cell` still SHOWS the column on a 1280 screen. Tables that must fit a 1280 laptop hide secondary columns with `2xl:` (1536). `components/ui/table.tsx` puts `whitespace-nowrap` on every cell — long text cells need `whitespace-normal` explicitly.
- [ ] Print templates (`lib/print/*` builders fed from PO, WO, vendor-return, material-issue pages) still carry English labels; the shared forms `components/forms/POForm.tsx` and `components/forms/ItemForm.tsx` were outside the sweep and may too.
- [ ] Numbers on the work-order pages use `toLocaleString(undefined, …)` — browser locale, so an English browser shows `1,234.5` beside Indonesian `Rp 1.234`.
- [ ] Local PostgreSQL has no `JubelioPushDefaults` row with `id = 'singleton'` (`packages/db/prisma/postgres/migrations/0_init` creates the table but never inserts it; the MariaDB migration does), so Jubelio → Pengaturan → push defaults cannot load or save locally. The card now says so instead of spinning forever.
- [ ] Not reviewed: the salesman/SPG PWA screens (`/pwa/*` redirect an admin to the backoffice) and mobile layouts of backoffice tables (they scroll sideways inside their cards).

### Ops / infra
- [ ] **`next dev` does NOT recompile `apps/web/app/globals.css` on change — the served CSS chunk keeps its old content AND its old hash.** Hit three times in one session (a `marquee` keyframe, the folder colour tokens, a `.keping-kode` rule): the rule is in the file, braces balance, type-check and lint pass, and the class simply has no effect in the browser. `touch`ing the file does not help; neither does a plain dev-server restart, nor `rm -rf .next/cache/webpack`. **Only `rm -rf apps/web/.next` followed by a restart works.** Diagnose it in one step — fetch the served stylesheet and grep for the new selector:
  `CSS=$(curl -s localhost:3000/login | grep -o '/_next/static/[^"]*\.css' | head -1); curl -s "localhost:3000$CSS" | grep -c '<selector>'`
  A `0` there with the rule present in `globals.css` is this bug, not a CSS mistake. Earlier additions stay compiled, so "some of my CSS works and the newest doesn't" is the signature.
- [ ] **After edits, `next dev` (Turbopack) can stay broken until it is restarted — even once the edits stop.** Symptoms in `web-dev.log`: `Failed to call useTranslations because the context from NextIntlClientProvider was not found` on random pages (HomeShell, StoreDetailShell, KlaimBaruClient…) and `module factory is not available`, both with HTTP 200 and the page falling back to client rendering. Rewriting the big `lib/i18n/messages/*.json` (the i18n merge script) triggers it most. It is NOT a code bug: on 2026-10-01 a fresh start (`pkill -f "next dev"`, `rm -rf apps/web/.next`, start) served 62 requests with 0 errors, while the same pages errored in a server that had seen edits. Restart before concluding anything from these errors.
- [ ] **A running `next dev` keeps the Prisma client it loaded at startup — regenerating does not reach it.** After a schema change, `corepack pnpm -F @elorae/db generate` rewrites `packages/db/dist`, and every vitest run picks the new client up (each run loads it fresh), but the dev server does not hot-reload `@elorae/db`: the first page that touches a new column fails with `PrismaClientValidationError: Unknown field \`<column>\` for select statement`. Green tests plus that error in the browser is the signature. Fix: restart the dev server — **`pkill -f "next dev"`, not `lsof -ti:3000 | xargs kill`**, because port 3000 also carries the Claude desktop app's own network process, and `~/.elorae-local/erp.sh stop` kills by port. Hit 2026-09-17 when `ColorPalette` gained `fabricHex`/`portions`.
- [x] DB backups — LIVE on prod since 2026-08-08. `scripts/backup-db.sh` installed on the VPS, cron 19:15 UTC (02:15 WIB), writing to the private `elorae-backups` R2 bucket. First run verified end-to-end: 91,471,524 bytes, decrypt-verified, upload size-confirmed, and the daily + monthly objects both exist. **Decryption with the off-site passphrase separately proven** — the password-manager copy (not the server's file) was tested against the live archive and decrypts it to a complete dump. AWS CLI is user-local at `~/.local/bin` because `awscli` is not apt-installable on this host, so the crontab MUST set `PATH` (PR #227).
- [ ] Nothing has yet proven the CRON invocation works — every successful run so far was manual. A cron-only failure (PATH, environment, working directory) would surface only in `/home/elorae/backup.log`. Check it once, then this is genuinely closed.
- [ ] No restore DRILL has been done. Decryption is proven; actually loading a dump into a scratch database and looking at it is not, and a dump can decrypt perfectly while failing to import (charset, a table the schema no longer has, an interrupted section that still ends with the trailer). The README documents the drill — run it once against a real archive, then again whenever the schema changes shape.
- [x] `--copy-props none` is required for any bucket-to-bucket `aws s3 cp` on R2: the default carries object tags across via `GetObjectTagging`, which R2 does not implement, so the copy fails `NotImplemented` while the source object is perfectly fine. Found on the first live backup run; the same trap awaits any future R2 copy code (PR #227).
- [ ] Backup failure is visible only in `backup.log` on the VPS — nothing renders it and nothing alerts. Same gap as the `AdminNotification` feed; a health-check ping or a "last successful backup" surface would close it.
- [ ] **GitHub Actions cache hits the hard 10 GB per-repo ceiling in about two days of pushes** — measured 2026-08-09 at 9.996 GiB across 123 entries, of which 9.35 GB had not been READ since 6–7 August while that day's runs touched only 91 MB. At the cap, every run evicts LRU entries, so the Docker layer cache thrashes rather than helping. Cause: `deploy.yml` exported with `cache-to: type=gha,mode=max` on both `scope=web` and `scope=api`, which writes every intermediate layer (pnpm store, `node_modules`, `.next`, Prisma engines) — largest single blobs 738 MB / 513 MB / 441 MB. Switched to `mode=min` (PR #230) and the stale entries were purged by hand; GitHub's own 7-day unused-eviction would not have cleared them until ~13–14 August. **Re-check `gh api /repos/.../actions/cache/usage` periodically** — if it climbs back to the cap, the next lever is dropping `scope=api` caching entirely, since the api image is the smaller of the two.
- [ ] **A wedged SSH tunnel presents as a Prisma pool timeout, not a network error** — cost 30 min on 2026-08-13. `ssh -fNL` opens the LOCAL listener immediately and keeps it open even after the master connection to the VPS goes deaf, so the port looks healthy in `ss -ltn`, raw TCP connects fine, and the only symptom is `pool timeout ... (pool connections: active=0 idle=0 limit=10)` — which reads like an app or connection-leak bug. **`active=0` is the tell: the socket never opened at all**, so it is never a leak. Confirm with `ss -tnp | grep <ssh-pid>` — a wedged tunnel shows the outbound `:22` connection `ESTAB` with a stuck non-zero **Send-Q** (data queued, never acked) and a pile of local `CLOSE-WAIT` entries behind it; a `mysql` client against the tunnel gives `ERROR 2013 ... waiting for initial communication packet, system error: 110`. Remedy is `kill <pid>` and reopen. **Always open the tunnel with `-o ServerAliveInterval=30 -o ServerAliveCountMax=3 -o ExitOnForwardFailure=yes`** so it tears itself down within ~90s of going deaf instead of leaving a zombie listener, and fails loudly at startup rather than listening-but-not-forwarding. Same `active=0` signature also appears when nothing is listening at all (the `:3308` dev bed with Docker down) — check the listener before blaming the tunnel.
- [ ] Auto-schedule the R2 orphan sweeper (not registered in `lib/cron/jobs.ts`) (PR #86).
- [ ] Queue observability: depth alert (AdminNotificationService) + dashboard panel; sweeper DEAD-letters after N attempts; prod-mode singleton smoke test (none in apps/api) (PR #73).
- [ ] VPS log shipping (Loki/Logtail) for off-server retention + backup Redis + Caddy cert volumes (PR #53).
- [ ] Trim web image size — drop unused Prisma engine binaries from the runtime layer (PR #56).
- [ ] Catalog sync: re-introduce the dropped progress log via an atomic counter (`catalog-sync.service.ts` logs only an end summary) (PR #39).

### Testing / infra
- [x] `packages/db` spec teardowns could truncate seven tables — every filter in all four DB-touching specs now goes through `seededId()` (`packages/db/src/spec-teardown.ts`), ids are declared `= ""` and reset at hook top, and `journal-writer.spec.ts` (found during review, one throw from wiping the whole `Journal` ledger) got the same treatment. Mutation-proven for the reservation-writer and backfill specs (PR #223).
- [ ] **SAME HAZARD STILL LIVE IN `apps/web` — the larger half.** 17–18 test files declare `let <x>Id: string;` and filter teardowns on it against the same `:3308` bed (e.g. `lib/finance/journal.test.ts:13-15`, `lib/purchasing/set-po-paid-at-action.test.ts:45-49`, `lib/field-sales/approve-action.test.ts:50-57`, `lib/field-sales/queries.test.ts:76-87`, `lib/finance/journals/mapping.test.ts:40-41` — that last one can delete the whole `ChartAccount` table). Plus two that over-match with NO variable ever being undefined: `app/pwa/stores/actions.spec.ts:64` deletes every store with a `TEST-`-prefixed code, and `lib/store-changes/writer.test.ts:32` deletes every `STORE_CHANGE_REQUEST` notification created since the test started — including ones a running dev app wrote. `packages/db` is done; do not read the class as closed (PR #223).
- [x] A lint rule flags **bare-variable id filters** (shorthand `{ itemId }` AND explicit `{ id: leafId }` alike) inside `deleteMany`/`updateMany` in `apps/web` spec/test files — `no-restricted-syntax` in `apps/web/eslint.config.mjs`. Passing the id through a guard call satisfies it. **Covers the undefined-collapse hazard only.** It does NOT catch a teardown that over-matches real rows with no variable involved (`{ code: { startsWith: "TEST-" } }`, a bare `createdAt` range) — those still need review judgement, and two live examples are listed above (PR #224).
- [ ] That rule is **WARN, not error**, because ~18 existing `apps/web` test files still violate it and a red `eslint .` helps nobody. **Flip it to `"error"` the moment that sweep lands** — a rule nobody must obey is decoration.
- [ ] The rule does not cover `packages/db`, which has no ESLint config and no eslint devDep (adding one means a new dependency + install). `packages/db` is clean today, so this is regression cover it lacks, not a live defect.
- [ ] (2026-10-06: `.github/workflows/ci.yml` runs type-check api + web, lint web and the DB-free guard tests on PRs to master — it only BLOCKS merges once the owner makes it a required status check, and apps/api has no eslint config) Neither CI workflow runs lint, tests or type-check — only `bugbit-pr-review.yml` and `deploy.yml` exist. So every check in this repo is opt-in on a developer's laptop, and the lint rule above only fires for someone who chooses to run it. A CI job is what makes any of it enforcement rather than advice.
- [ ] Resetting fixture ids at hook top (PR #223) removed master's accidental self-healing: ids used to carry over, so a partially-failed teardown got a second attempt on the next test. Now a partial teardown leaks permanently and, where the leaked row collides with a describe-scoped unique code, every remaining test in the file fails `P2002`. Correct trade — a leak beats a truncation — but it is why `:3308` currently holds 4 orphan `TEST-UOM-TEST-FS-*` rows with no matching Item, from four separate past runs. Worth a one-off cleanup.
- [ ] `apps/web/lib/finance/journals/mapping-test-fixture.ts:26-36` — `restoreMappings` loops the four roles unguarded, so a throw on the first leaves the rest of the live GL config pointing at throwaway test accounts. This is the fixture the hardened specs depend on for their "restore shared config first" step, so the one step that must never be skipped can still half-fail (found by PR #222's whole-branch review).
- [ ] Sales-sweep specs write the cron's own arming switch (`SystemSetting["finance.glCutoverDate"]`) onto the shared bed. Teardown restores it first and is step-isolated, but a run KILLED between `beforeEach` and teardown leaves `:3308` armed at the test value — and dev has all four sales roles mapped with 205 eligible orders, so the dev cron would then post ~410 journals, after which the next run snapshots the leaked value as the real config. Only closed properly by DB-spec isolation (PR #222).
- [ ] DB-spec isolation: `vitest.config.ts` `fileParallelism:false` serializes the WHOLE suite (slow). Scope DB specs into a serial project, or add a tx-rollback harness for the ~20 real-row specs.
- [ ] `apps/web/lib/finance/settlement/journal.test.ts`'s `afterEach` is order-fragile: a failure partway through leaves stale ids so a delete throws and aborts the hook before later cleanup, leaking a row into the shared dev database.
- [ ] DB specs mutate the operator's real `JournalAccountMapping` on the shared `:3308` bed; a `beforeAll` that exceeds its timeout leaves a zombie hook that can re-point live GL config after `afterAll` has already restored it. Mitigated (single `beforeAll` window, `afterAll` net, loud restore failure) but only closed properly by DB-spec isolation — a dedicated schema or a tx-rollback harness. Note it caused a real incident on 2026-08-05.
- [ ] `apps/web/lib/finance/settlement/journal.test.ts` still seeds 8 chart accounts and 8 mappings in `beforeEach` with an unguarded `afterEach`, the same shape as the fixture that failed — give it the `beforeAll` + guarded-`afterAll` treatment next time that file is opened.
- [x] `supplier-payment-journal.test.ts` reused `docNumber` suffixes across cases on `@unique` columns, so a partial teardown surfaced as a unique-constraint failure in a LATER, unrelated test — all 63 literals now come from a describe-scoped counter, so the collision can't be hand-reintroduced (PR #220).
- [ ] Same hazard one table over in that file: the alternate `chartAccount.code` literals (`9${token}9/7/6`) are still hand-numbered on a `@unique` column (PR #220).
- [ ] `apps/web/lib/field-sales/writer.test.ts` and `apps/web/lib/field-sales/approve-action.test.ts` still carry ~20 bare-variable teardown filters with no `seededId()` — the same undefined-collapse hazard the lint rule warns about. The delivery specs (`delivery/writer.test.ts`, `delivery/queries.test.ts`, `reservation-writer.delivery.spec.ts`) are clean; these two were not swept (feat/field-sales-delivery).

## What NOT to do

- Don't put EPIC-XX refs in commits, PRs, branch names, or shared specs.
- Don't bundle multiple sub-projects into one PR. Each sub-project is its own slice.
- Don't write to web-owned tables from apps/api (and vice versa) without going through a `@elorae/db` helper — see `docs/BOUNDARY.md §3`.
- Don't add Prisma model comments. Don't add `Co-Authored-By` trailers to commits.
- Don't run `prisma migrate dev` against the shared VPS MariaDB — that creates throwaway migrations and resets state. Use `migrate:deploy` only.
- Don't deploy apps/api or apps/web to Vercel. Both need persistent processes; Vercel functions don't fit (node-cron, BullMQ workers). Production target is the Hostinger VPS; local-ngrok is the dev/demo fallback.
- **Don't run the whole test suite** (no filter, or repo-wide). It's slow + wasteful. Scope to the specs you changed. **api (jest):** `pnpm -F @elorae/api test -- <pattern> [<pattern>...]` (jest treats each positional arg as a testPathPattern, OR-ed). **web (vitest):** `pnpm -F @elorae/web exec vitest run <pattern> [<pattern>...]` — positional args are file-path filters. **DO NOT use `pnpm -F @elorae/web test -- <pattern>`** — the `--` pattern is NOT forwarded to vitest, so it silently runs the FULL ~450-test suite against `:3308` (confirmed 2026-07-26; the jest-style `test --` form only works for api). Only widen if a change plausibly affects unrelated specs.
- **Never write an id filter as identifier shorthand in a spec teardown.** `deleteMany({ where: { itemId } })` collapses to `deleteMany({})` — deleting the WHOLE TABLE on the shared `:3308` bed — whenever the fixture hook threw before assigning that id, because **Prisma drops an `undefined` filter term** (measured: `item.count({ where: { id: undefined } })` returns every row). Declare fixture ids `= ""`, reset them at the top of the hook, and pass every one through `seededId()` from `@elorae/db`'s `src/spec-teardown.ts` so the filter is explicit. Prefer `delete` over `deleteMany` for a single row where you can — `delete` REJECTS an undefined id instead of widening, which is fail-closed. Same rule for `in: [...]` lists (`?? []`). Never filter a teardown on a prefix, a category or a date range alone: those over-match real rows without any variable being undefined.
- **A test-bed spec that calls a DB-WIDE function must scope it to the spec's own seeded rows.** The tests share `:3308` with real dev data. A function that sweeps/batches over ALL matching rows (e.g. `postPendingSalesJournals`, any future reconcile/backfill sweep) will mutate REAL rows when a spec invokes it — e.g. the sales-journal sweep test journaled real orders to the test's chart accounts, then teardown FK-failed and leaked. Give such functions an optional scope param (`orderIds`/filter) and pass it from the spec; a single-seeded-row test masks the global reach in review. Learned 2026-07-26 (EPIC-13-03).
- **Don't type-check the whole repo** (turbo/all-package). Scope to the one package you changed: `pnpm -F @elorae/api type-check` only. (`@elorae/web` type-check stays the user's — it saturates disk; never run it.) tsc can't do single-file, so package-scope is the finest partial available.

## When you need more context

- **Architecture/data ownership:** `docs/BOUNDARY.md`.
- **EPIC story details + status:** the GitHub project board — each EPIC has a tracking issue (`EPIC-NN` → issue #`NN+4`, e.g. EPIC-13 → #17, EPIC-15 → #19). `gh issue view <n>`. Update the issue's progress checklist on merge (the maintenance rule now applies to the board, not a local file).
- **Past designs/plans:** `docs/superpowers/specs/` and `docs/superpowers/plans/` (local-only, gitignored).
- **What changed and why:** `git log --oneline` (commit messages are descriptive; bodies are rare by convention).
